# Field inventory: top-venue papers 2024-2026 on tracking and reconstruction of humans manipulating objects

Built 2026-10-01 by workflow wf_ce41fbb3-582 (47 scouts: one per venue-year, two per subfield, five curated-list harvesters), merged by arXiv id and normalised title. Companion JSON: field_papers_2026-10-01.json. This is step 1 of the field-first study; the pain-point extraction (step 2) and the field map (step 3) follow.

## Scope

Subfields: S1 hand-object interaction pose and reconstruction; S2 whole-body human-object interaction capture; S3 bimanual and two-hand tracking and datasets; S4 human pose and motion capture under occlusion from single RGB-D, RGB or sparse sensors; S5 object pose tracking in the hand or under occlusion; S6 human demonstration capture for robot learning; S7 real-time avatars, digital twins, telepresence and single-camera clinical motion analysis. Venues: CVPR, ICCV, ECCV, NeurIPS, ICLR, SIGGRAPH, SIGGRAPH Asia, CoRL, RSS, ICRA, IROS, T-RO, TPAMI, IJCV, 2024-2026; datasets back to 2022; arXiv 2025-2026 preprints aimed at these venues are tagged arXiv.

## Access caveats (read before trusting completeness)

The session had a WebSearch budget of 200 calls, spent by the first twelve scouts, and the egress proxy blocked arxiv.org, openaccess.thecvf.com, ecva.net, openreview.net, dl.acm.org, roboticsproceedings.org, papercept, IEEE Xplore, Semantic Scholar, dblp and papers.cool for direct fetches. Most scouts therefore worked from GitHub mirrors: the Paper Copilot accepted-paper lists (CVPR 2024-2026, ICCV 2025, ECCV 2024, NeurIPS 2024-2025, ICLR 2025-2026, full lists with abstracts), the PMLR source repositories for CoRL 2024-2025, a scraped ICRA 2026 program, and curated awesome lists. Coverage is therefore near-complete for the CVF, NeurIPS, ICLR and CoRL venues, moderate for ICRA and IROS, and a lower bound for SIGGRAPH, RSS, T-RO, TPAMI and IJCV. Every entry was seen by its scout in a fetched page or search result; 556 of the 1563 entries were found independently by two or more scouts. Per-scout notes are in the coverage table at the end.

## Counts

Unique papers: 1563. With arXiv id: 975. With code link: 646.

Per subfield (a paper can carry several tags): S1 354, S2 149, S3 207, S4 439, S5 269, S6 496, S7 254

Per venue-year (top 30): CVPR 2024 110, CVPR 2025 101, arXiv 2026 99, ICRA 2026 95, ICCV 2025 81, IROS 2025 81, CVPR 2026 77, ECCV 2024 70, arXiv 2025 67, ECCV 2026 59, ICRA 2025 55, IROS 2024 54, NeurIPS 2024 44, ICRA 2024 42, NeurIPS 2025 39, CoRL 2025 39, ICLR 2026 37, CoRL 2024 34, ICLR 2025 31, TPAMI 2025 27, SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 26, RSS 2026 24, SIGGRAPH 2025 19, RSS 2025 16, SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 15, RSS 2024 15, SIGGRAPH 2026 13, TPAMI 2024 13, arXiv 2024 11, TPAMI 2026 10

## Coverage notes per scout

| Source | Entries | Queries or fetches | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| CVPR 2024 | 101 | 18 | I could not reach openaccess.thecvf.com directly with curl because the egress proxy blocked it. Instead I used the Paper Copilot CVPR 2024 list on GitHub, which holds 2716 main-conference papers (the official accepted count is about 2719), each with its abstract and openaccess link, and searched it locally. I then cross-checked against web searches and two curated GitHub lists. Every CVPR 2024 entry in the hand-pose list (24 papers) and the HOI list was already in the result. The last two web searches added nothing new. Every paper listed appears in that full list with an openaccess.thecvf.com CVPR2024 URL, so verified=true means the paper is confirmed as a CVPR 2024 main-conference paper. Workshop papers (CVPRW) are not included.  Some fields are less certain than others: - Fields from abstracts: the contribution and sensing fields come from the abstracts. - Datasets marked "(per paper)": these are from my own knowledge, were not seen in the abstract, and should be spot-checked. - Missing arXiv IDs and code URLs: about 30 papers have none, and these were left empty rather than guessed. - The digital-twins paper: "Neural Implicit Representation for Building Digital Twins of Unknown Articulated Objects" had no abstract in the source, so its contribution is from my knowledge.  Scope decisions: - Generic 3D human pose and human-object interaction (HOI) detection papers were left out. This covers plain HPE lifting and HMR backbones such as TokenHMR, KTPFormer and AiOS, and 2D HOI detection or recognition. - Text-to-motion and human-scene generation without capture were left out, as were head-only avatar papers that are not depth- or consumer-sensor based. - Robot grasp-synthesis papers with no human component were left out, for example Dexterous Grasp Transformer and the language-driven grasp detectors. - Generic RGB-D SLAM and depth estimation were left out. - Category-level 6D pose papers, together with REAL275 and HouseCat6D, were included under S5 as neighbouring work. - A search summary called "AdvDex" a CVPR 2024 paper. It is not in the CVPR 2024 list (its arXiv preprint is from 2026), so it was left out. - A curated list labels "Object-Centric Dexterous Manipulation from Human Motion Data" as CVPR 2024. It is not in the CVPR 2024 list either, so it was left out.  I am highly confident the list is complete for S1, S3 and S5 at CVPR 2024. Coverage of S4 and S7 is reasonable but depends on judgement, because those subfields overlap heavily with general human-pose and avatar work. |
| CVPR 2025 | 94 | 25 | I could not reach openaccess.thecvf.com, cvpr.thecvf.com or papers.cool directly because the egress proxy blocked them. Instead I screened the complete CVPR 2025 main-track list from the Papercopilot paperlists JSON on GitHub: 2,872 papers with titles, abstracts, openaccess URLs and arXiv IDs, against the official count of roughly 2,878. A small number of main-conference papers may therefore be missing, and workshop papers are not included because that source does not list them.  I filtered titles and abstracts by keyword in three passes and read the abstracts of about 110 candidates. I then cross-checked three curated GitHub lists (Zhenhao-Zhang awesome-hand-object-interaction-learning, haonanhe Hand-Object-Interaction, SeanChenxy Hand3DResearch) and ran web searches. All CVPR 2025 entries in those lists were already found or were deliberately excluded. The last four web searches added nothing new.  Every listed paper appears in the CVPR 2025 source data or an openaccess or virtual-site search result, so verified=true. For three papers the JSON had no abstract, and I confirmed them by web search: TASTE-Rob, ONDA-Pose, and Learning Physics-Based Full-Body Human Reaching and Grasping. Some arXiv IDs and code links came from the curated lists or search results. An arXiv ID is left empty when no source showed it. The datasets field records only what the abstract names; much evaluation data is reported only in the paper bodies, which I could not read.  Coverage is high for S1, S3 and S5 (novel-object and hand-held pose) and for S2. It is good for S4. S6 is thin because most robot-learning papers at CVPR 2025 do not use human demonstrations. I left out pure robot grasping and policy papers that do not use human data: DexGrasp Anything, UniGraspTransformer, DexHandDiff, KStar Diffuser, PDFactor, Tra-MoE and ZeroGrasp.  S7 is deliberately selective. CVPR 2025 has many more head and body avatar papers, and I kept only those that are real-time, on-device, telepresence-oriented or built from consumer or sparse sensors. I found no single-camera clinical upper-limb paper; the in-bed monitoring papers are tagged S7.  I also excluded category-level object pose papers (GCE-Pose, GIVEPose, the stereo category-level paper, CAP-Net, the correspondence-based category-level paper), HOI detection papers, and HOI image or video generation papers that do not address 3D capture, such as HOIGen-1M and PersonaHOI.  Accented author names are written in ASCII (Alpar Cseke, Guenole Fiche, Lea Muller). |

Completeness is moderate. Search-engine sampling of an approximately 4,090-paper conference likely misses some relevant posters, especially in S4 (generic human mesh recovery) and S5 (generic 6D pose). The last two discovery queries added nothing new, which met the stopping rule. |
| ICCV 2025 | 78 | 21 | Direct access to openaccess.thecvf.com, iccv.thecvf.com, papers.cool, arXiv and Semantic Scholar was blocked by the egress proxy. Instead I used the Paper Copilot ICCV 2025 list on GitHub (papercopilot/paperlists iccv2025.json): 2728 entries with titles, authors, abstracts and openaccess links, which is consistent with the roughly 2700 ICCV 2025 main-track papers. I ran keyword searches over every title and abstract and screened the hits by hand. I cross-checked against the 52CV curated ICCV 2025 list (2797 titles). Fewer than 90 of its titles were missing from the main list, and those were section headers, workshop items or title variants. None of the missing ones was in S1-S7. Ten web searches turned up no ICCV 2025 paper that was not already in the main list; the last four added nothing. One search summary claimed BIGS was at ICCV 2025, but it is a CVPR 2025 paper and is excluded. Every listed paper has an ICCV 2025 openaccess page in the source list, so verified=true. Its contribution, sensing and datasets fields come from the abstract only; "not named in abstract" means I did not read the full paper. The G-DexGrasp dataset (AffordPose) comes from a search-result summary. code_url is filled only where the abstract or the 52CV list gives a link. Two pairs of duplicate entries in the source were merged: "Generalizable 4D HOI Synthesis by Composing Interaction Primitives" is the same paper as PrimHOI, and "Inter Inertial Poser" is the same paper as Group Inertial Poser. Deliberate exclusions: (a) 2D HOI detection papers, which are not 3D capture: Bilateral Collaboration with LVLMs, HOLa, No More Sibling Rivalry, Open-Vocabulary HOI Detection with Interaction-aware Prompt, Visual Relation Diffusion for HOI Detection, P3HOT human-object contact prediction; (b) robot manipulation policies, world models and VLA papers with no human-demonstration capture component, such as A0, RoboBridge, GWM, iManip, Moto, EC-Flow, VLABench and FedVLA; (c) text-to-motion, dance and head-only avatar papers; (d) general point tracking and 4D reconstruction without a human or hand focus, such as St4RTrack and CoTracker3. Some tagging is generous: robot bimanual policy papers are tagged S3/S6, generic novel- and category-level object pose papers are tagged S5, and single-image body avatars are tagged S7. ICCV 2025 workshop papers (ICCVW) are listed separately from the main conference and were not included. I am fairly confident the list is close to complete for main-track ICCV 2025 papers in S1-S7. The likeliest misses are papers whose title and abstract avoid all of my keywords. |
| ECCV 2024 | 66 | 14 | Main index: the Paper Copilot mirror of the ECVA ECCV 2024 proceedings (2387 main-track papers with abstracts and ecva.net open-access links). ecva.net, papers.cool, arXiv, Semantic Scholar, OpenAlex and dblp were all blocked by the egress proxy, so the official site could not be opened directly. I screened every title, then the abstracts, by keyword and by dataset name. Of 2387 papers, 66 are in scope. The two hand curated lists (awesome-hand-pose-estimation, Hand3DResearch) added nothing new, which suggests S1 and S3 coverage is close to complete. The search phase stopped early: the session hit its 200-call WebSearch budget. As a result, arXiv IDs and code links are filled only where a search result showed them. They are blank for FreeZe, PACE, AvatarPose, MANIKIN, EgoBody3M and many S4/S7 papers, and I did not fill any from memory. Where a URL appeared only in the abstract or a curated list, it is recorded as given; for some papers this is a project page rather than a repository. Inclusion judgments: (1) No ECCV 2024 main-track paper is a dedicated bimanual-object tracking dataset; S3 coverage is mainly HANDS23 (ARCTIC), AttentionHand and HandFormer. (2) Generic monocular human mesh recovery papers were left out unless they target occlusion, world grounding, real time, uncertainty or sparse sensors. Left out on this basis: VQ-HPS, Global-to-Pixel, Multi-RoI HMR, NC-RetNet, 3DSA, Rotated Orthographic Projection. (3) Category-level object pose papers without an occlusion, tracking or novel-object focus were left out: GS-Pose, LaPose, U-COPE, OP-Align. (4) Robot-only grasp and policy papers with no human data were left out: UGG, Learning Cross-hand Policies, ManiGaussian, MaxMI. (5) Also left out: text-to-motion generation, two-person reaction synthesis, radar/WiFi pose estimation, and the head-only avatar papers other than the telepresence-focused ones. ECCV 2024 workshop papers (e.g., the HANDS workshop ARCTIC challenge reports and Springer workshop volumes) appear in separate proceedings, not with the main conference, so they are excluded. verified=true means the title and authors were seen in the ECVA-derived list, and for most papers also in a search result. |

I did not open the official poster pages themselves. About half the entries have a contribution, sensing and dataset text taken from an abstract or README I actually read. Possible wrong IDs: some arXiv IDs come from project pages and could point to an earlier version. Human Mesh Modeling for Anny Body uses the naver/anny README ID 2511.03589. | ICLR 2026 | 36 | 18 | Direct access to iclr.cc, openreview.net, papers.cool, arxiv.org, Semantic Scholar and dblp was blocked by the egress proxy, and the session WebSearch budget was already used up. Coverage therefore comes from the Paper Copilot dataset (github.com/papercopilot/paperlists, iclr/iclr2026.json, fetched via media.githubusercontent.com). It mirrors OpenReview decisions for all 19,814 ICLR 2026 submissions, 5,358 of them accepted (5,120 poster, 223 oral, 15 conditional), and includes titles, abstracts and keywords. Four keyword passes over the accepted titles and abstracts found the candidates, and each abstract was read before inclusion. Every listed paper has an accepted status in that dataset and its OpenReview forum URL. All are posters; none were orals. Five curated GitHub lists were checked. Only two added ICLR 2026 entries: EgoHandICL (faccoco) and ViPRA plus RIGVid (IRMVLab). These gave arXiv IDs and code links, but no new papers; the last two passes added nothing new. Limitations: (1) arXiv IDs and code URLs are filled only when a fetched curated list showed them, otherwise left empty. (2) Dataset names come from the abstracts only, so 'not named in abstract' means the abstract was silent, not that no dataset was used. (3) The Paper Copilot file has only the main track, so workshop papers are not covered. (4) Borderline papers were excluded on purpose: pure robot policies (TwinVLA, H3DP, DemoGrasp), video-generation-for-robotics papers (MIMIC, RoboMaster), human-human interaction generation, humanoid control from text, 2D generic object tracking, image object insertion (SpatialHand). (5) S7 avatar papers were included generously. (6) S5 is thin: no ICLR 2026 paper on in-hand 6-DoF object tracking was found; only RIGVid uses a 6D pose tracker. Confidence that main-track coverage is complete is fairly high for papers whose abstracts use standard terms. Moderate for papers that describe relevant work in unusual wording. |

| SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 | 25 | 16 | I could not reach any official source directly. WebSearch had already used up the session quota of 200 searches. (1) Two complete SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 indexes hosted there: chang-xinhai/AI-Conference-Paper-Lists, a Crossref/DBLP harvest of 593 records (146 conference papers, 122 TOG journal-track papers, plus posters, technical communications, XR, Emerging Technologies and other tracks), and papercopilot/paperlists, 265 technical papers taken from the official program. The two indexes agree apart from title variants, so I am highly confident that coverage of the technical-paper track (about 268 papers) is complete. (2) Per-paper summaries for all 265 technical papers (the woAIxuexiSR notes), plus GitHub code search for arXiv IDs and code links. Every listed paper appears in at least one of these venue indexes, so verified=true means the title, authors and DOI were seen in a venue listing. It does not mean I read the publisher page. Contribution sentences for the technical papers are based on the per-paper summaries. Three notes were empty: Measuring Human Motion Under Clothing (sensing taken from index tags only), Gaussian Surfel Splatting (sensing and dataset not confirmed) and Geometry-Aware Retargeting (excluded). The seven posters and technical communications are described from their titles only, and each says so in its contribution. They are listed with the conference but are not full papers, and the orchestrator may want to drop them. Inclusion decisions: DiffH2O, LINGO and Synchronize Dual Hands are synthesis papers and are tagged as neighbours. I excluded PDP, Robot Motion Diffusion Model, Geometry-Aware Retargeting for Two-Skinned Characters, head-avatar papers without a consumer-sensor or telepresence angle (NPGA, GGHead, GaussianHeads, TextToon), Millimetric Human Surface Capture and the volumetric-video streaming papers as outside S1-S7. 'Reconstructing Close Human Interactions from Multiple Views' (Shuai et al.) is labelled SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 in Foruck/Awesome-Human-Motion, but it is absent from both venue indexes (likely SIGGRAPH Asia 2023), so I excluded it. arXiv IDs come from GitHub-hosted arXiv daily lists or project READMEs; I found none for Measuring Human Motion Under Clothing or Gaussian Surfel Splatting. No SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 technical paper on RGB-D hand-object pose tracking, in-hand 6-DoF object tracking (S5) or robot learning from human demonstration (S6) was found in the venue index. |

It is not a check against IEEE Xplore.  Other limits: - RA-L papers presented at ICRA 2024 do not appear in the atlas and are not covered. All other ids are left empty rather than guessed, and those entries link to the atlas cluster file. - Contribution sentences come from the titles plus the abstracts or READMEs I could fetch. One of them (Awesome-Dexterous-Manipulation) gives wrong venue labels for several older papers, so its entries were not used.  Inclusion choices: - Excluded as out of scope: robot-hand grasp synthesis, skeleton-based action recognition, and robot-side bimanual planning. - Included because the subfield definitions invite generous tagging: marker and event-camera object tracking (S5) and VR or digital-twin teleoperation systems (S7). Some entries were seen as a title only, so their first author, sensing setup and datasets are blank or marked not verified (Pose-Driven Compression, EGCN++, HandRT, Keystate-Driven). For journal extensions without their own arXiv entry, arxiv_id is left empty, and dataset details were taken from the conference abstract and marked not re-verified. Each entry's year is the issue year where known; otherwise it is the early-access year. Some papers are borderline for this map: monocular human mesh recovery and object 6D pose without hands were tagged S4 or S5 generously. I left out 2D pose, HOI detection, text-to-motion, and studio-only datasets. I also left out one paper whose topic I could not confirm: Equivariant Diffusion Model With A5-Group Neurons (TPAMI.2025.3540593). Overall the list is fairly complete for 2024 and mid-2025 and for July-September 2026, and incomplete for late 2025 to early 2026. |

Every listed paper appeared in one of those lists or in a repository result or README, so verified=true means the paper exists as seen there. It does not mean the venue was checked against official proceedings. Venue sources: repository descriptions, README BibTeX, and the curated lists' venue tags. Exceptions: UMI (RSS 2024), UMI on Legs (CoRL 2024), Open-TeleVision (CoRL 2024; also stated in its repository description) and EgoBridge (NeurIPS 2025; from the IRMV list tag). For UMI and UMI on Legs the venue comes from background knowledge; their READMEs do not state it. First authors marked 'not confirmed', or given only a surname, could not be read from any reachable page; for several other authors (EgoBridge, R+X, ZeroMimic, Motion Tracks, HuDOR, Point Policy, Robot See Robot Do, Track2Act, HRP, Vid2Robot, Chain-of-Modality, MimicDroid, DemoDiffusion, Dexterity from Smart Lenses, Phantom, Masquerade, OKAMI, ScrewMimic, ViViDex, Humanoid Policy) the name comes from background knowledge, not a fetched page. Titles for several 2026 arXiv items (C2Dex, Dex-X, RoboWheel, MINT/EgoPipeline, SEED-UMI, DexDirect, Lucid-XR, ACE-Data-0) are paraphrased from the curated table's short name plus description, because the full title was not reachable. Being-H0 is listed at ICML 2026 per its README, although ICML is not among the target venues. MimicFunc's arXiv id follows the IRMV list (2508.13534); its repository README links 2502.11744 (FUNCTO), so treat that id as uncertain. I deliberately left out mostly-latent-action, world-model and VLA-pretraining papers from the IRMV list (e.g. UniVLA, Moto, LAPA, villa-X, Motus), as well as pure robot teleoperation hardware (ALOHA, Mobile ALOHA, GELLO, HOMIE), as outside the capture and retargeting focus of S6. 2024 preprints with no confirmed venue (FastUMI, Bunny-VisionPro) were also left out. HOI capture datasets such as OakInk2, HO-Cap, HOT3D and TACO belong to the S1/S3 scouts. Completeness: good for high-visibility 2024-2025 RSS/CoRL/ICRA/CVPR systems that have GitHub repositories. Weak for ICRA/IROS papers without code, for T-RO and RA-L, and for 2026 venue acceptances that only proceedings pages would show. The last two GitHub queries in a row added nothing in scope. |

The venue items found (TULIP, Care-PD, InclusiveVidPose, ProGait) are Parkinson's, gait or limb-deficiency datasets, not upper-limb studies. The upper-limb items are 2026 arXiv validation preprints (ARAT markerless mocap, reachable workspace, OpenCap Monocular, QMT). They are journal-style and not clearly aimed at the listed venues, so treat them as borderline. Confidence: moderate for 2024-2025 CVPR/ICCV/ECCV/NeurIPS/ICLR/SIGGRAPH(Asia) coverage of real-time, telepresence and consumer-sensor avatars; low for SIGGRAPH 2024, robotics venues and journals. |

How the venues were checked: - Verified against the saved proceedings and program indexes: most CoRL 2024/2025, RSS 2024/2025, ICRA 2025/2026, IROS 2024/2025 and CVPR 2024/2025 entries. - Taken from the awesome list or the project page only, not checked against proceedings: UniVLA (RSS 2025), Track2Act (ECCV 2024), the zero-shot human interaction plans paper (ICRA 2024), AR-VRM and DexH2R (ICCV 2025), and the CVPR 2026 entries UniDex, JALA and VIPA-VLA. - Two venues come from project pages only: HERMES (T-RO 2026) and DexMachina (ICML 2026). - In-N-On is listed as CoRL by the awesome list but was not found in the CoRL index, so it is marked arXiv. TypeTele is in the CoRL 2025 index even though one list says IROS 2025. The "Human Demonstration Video as a Prompt" paper is in the ICRA 2026 program, not CoRL.  How the other fields were filled: - Sensing and dataset fields come from the fetched abstracts and pages. Where a page gave no detail, the field says so or says "authors" rather than guessing. - The first author of HiFi-UMI appears in the index only as the organisation "Simple AI". - code_url is filled only for github.com repositories seen in the lists or READMEs.  Deliberately left out: - Generic tools and representation-only works on the lists: point trackers, 3D generation and latent-action VLAs without hand or human data. - Arxiv-only items whose first author or venue could not be confirmed.  Confidence: high that every listed work exists and that the metadata matches what was seen. Coverage is moderate. It is complete for the human-video sections of the four lists fetched, but other awesome lists (for example wxpzhou and Kami-code) could not be retrieved, and per-venue searches could not be run. |
